Remarks.&mdash;During October the highest reading of the barometer was 30.334, on the 19th; and the lowest 29.870, on the 10th. The highest temperature was 92&deg;, on the 7th; and the lowest 6o&deg;, on the 30th and 31st. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 28th, averaging 13.7 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 2 days, measuring 0.02 inch. During November the highest reading of the barometer was 30.484, on the 26th; and the lowest 29.842, on the 23rd. The highest temperature was 87&deg;, on the 18th; and the lowest 430, on the 28th. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 25th, averaging 17.3 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 5 days, measuring 0.28 inch. During December the highest reading of the barometer was 30.570, on the 13th; and the lowest 30.100, on the 25th. The highest temperature was 720, on the 28th and 31st; and the lowest 36&deg;, on the 14th. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 12th, averaging 17.8 miles an hour during 24 hours; one day (the 31st) was calm throughout. Rain fell on 3 days, measuring 0.22 inch. IX. PART.IV., NOTIFICATIONSP During the Quarter ended December 31, 1892. 21
NOTICES,-TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 261. KIUNOCHOW DISTRICT. BUOYAGE OF HAINN STRAIT. NoTiE is hereby given that the under-mentioned buoys have been laid down in H nan Strait. MIDDIE CHANNEL. Middle Chand Bwoy.-A ten-foot conical buoy, painted in red and back horizon ands and surmounted by a black spherical cage, moored in 13 fathomns. Approximate position:- Latitude.................20 1735&quot; N. Iongitude................ 1o* 58&#39; 2&#39; E. This buoy marks the fairway of the eastern entrance to the Middle Channel. SOUTH CHANNEL. Magpie Rock Boy.-A six-foot conical black buoy, surmounted by a black triangu age, moored in 8 fathoms about 2 cables to the eastward of the rock and about 14 miles he south-eastward of Hainan Point. This buoy must be left on the port hand by vesse ntering the Strait from the eastward. Hainan Head Bank Bwoy.-A six-foot conical red buoy, surmounted by a black invert ustum cage, moored in 4 fathoms about 9 cables N. 67 E. from Hainan Point. This bu ust be left on the starboard hand by vessels entering the Strait from the eastward. Hainan Reef Buoy.-A six-foot conical black buoy, surmounted by a black spheric age, moored in z2 fathoms about 21 cables to the northward of the outer rock of Hainan Re hich is marked on the British Admiralty Chart No. 876, &quot; dries 4 feet.&quot; This buoy must left on the port hand by vessels entering the Strait from the eastward. The bearings given are magnetic and the depths are for low water of spring tides.
DIRECIONS, With the buoys in the positions above described, the best track for vessels toflowwe usiag the South Channel is to Pass 3 cables eastward of the Magpie Rook Buoy andte te to pass the Hainan Head Bank Buoy at about the same distance, taking care togwraais being set towards the shore, and remembering that it is safe to borrow towards tebnso the starboard hand, of which the lead, which should be kept going, will give warnn,wieo the other &#39;side the bottom is rooky and uneven and the lead gives no warning. TeHia Reef Buoy may be rounded at a distance of i cable or more at discretion, SH&amp;amp;xNA, s7th December 1892. A*

APPENDIX II. KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. QCTOBER-DEOEMBER 1892.
&amp;lt;g*$f V 192 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [OCT.-DEC., (Received 16th January 1893J CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, 7th January 1893. SIR, I.&mdash;a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the fourth quarter of the year 1892, October-December. b. The Custom House (&quot;Jh ff| 11) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.Tk 30 and Hk.Tk 80 per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon 7jC P^)&amp;gt; Changchow (J| ^j), Fotochow mmm), and Kowloon |j|) stations. c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II. MOVEMENTS OF VESSELS.&mdash;The number and movements of vessels, inwards and outwards, during the quarter, are set forth in the following Tables:&mdash; i&deg;. CAPSUIMOON. JUNKS. October. November. / 1892.] LAPPA. 203 (Received 21st January 1893J CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, 1 ith January 1893. SIR, I.&mdash;a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the fourth quarter of the year 1892, October-December. b. The Custom House &sect;!) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.Tfs. 30 and Hk.Tk 80 per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com&not; menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow Wi !JH) and Chienshan (fjfj OJ) stations, with three sub-stations, known as the Shekkok (%] Kuttai fc), and Kwanchiap pfj]) sub-stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
